- **Performance and complexity:** Configuration validation and reload occur once per worker, task signature selection occurs once per callback construction, and `event_object` rejection occurs before startup. Enabled-level map lookup is faster than the current list scan, JSON bypasses Symfony formatting, and no request/coroutine path gains a container lookup, lock, retry, yield, registry, or retained request state. The only accepted added work is the small conditional escape check for enabled line output required to preserve literal user values.
- **Laravel-facing result:** Core is Hyperf-derived low-level Swoole infrastructure rather than a Laravel-ported package. Laravel API parity is not applicable; PSR-3 behavior becomes conformant, and the owner approved the narrower Hypervel-specific raw Swoole configuration surface.
- **Implementation:** The default stdout logger now reloads validated worker configuration after Foundation's stable-repository rebuild, uses a precomputed level map, performs safe PSR interpolation and conditional line escaping, and emits resilient raw JSON with informative top-level object and resource markers. Task callbacks select Swoole's exact legacy or native-object signature and finish boundary from all dedicated settings. Global and per-port settings reject incompatible truthy `event_object` values at their complete mutation boundaries while allowing explicit false. Receive data is narrowed to string; the three dead public classes and false Coroutine dependency are removed; and package provenance, the direct Swoole requirement, shipped defaults, and user guidance are current.
- **Regression tests:** Stdout coverage exercises safe scalar, array, resource, object, date, invalid-UTF-8, recursive, and throwing-serializer context; literal Symfony markup and percent-bearing tags; raw JSON; custom, disabled, invalid, and non-string levels; invalid formats; and failed-reload atomicity. Worker-start coverage proves reload ordering before startup output and readiness while custom loggers remain untouched. Task coverage proves legacy construction and completion, every native-object setting, and legacy-alias precedence; a live-server test solely for the final native `Task::finish()` call was rejected as disproportionate because the final extension class has no honest unit seam and Swoole source defines that ownership boundary. Server coverage proves construction-time and later global/per-port `event_object` rejection, port-specific diagnostics, explicit-false acceptance, and the shipped disabled coroutine-task default.
- **Validation and review:** The focused affected group passes with 81 tests and 229 assertions. PHP CS Fixer changes none of 5,580 files; both PHPStan configurations pass; the complete components suite passes with 23,410 tests, 66,742 assertions, and 1,603 expected skips; Testbench passes with 347 tests, 1,031 assertions, and 3 expected skips; and dogfood passes with four tests and seven assertions. Root and split Composer validation, PHP syntax checks, `git diff --check`, broad stale-reference and settings-writer scans, a fresh full-diff caller/callee, lifecycle, API, documentation, hot-path, and overengineering review, and independent code review are complete. The final review improved top-level resource JSON normalization and signed off with no remaining issue.
- **Assessment:** All eight verified Core findings are fixed at their lowest owning boundary without a compatibility shim, dual callback adapter, per-log configuration lookup, synchronization layer, recursive normalizer, or speculative test seam. Cold boot and callback-construction paths own the new validation and selection work; ordinary logging uses a faster level lookup and only the approved small escaping guard. The result contains no stale surface, retained request state, hot-path regression, workaround, or unresolved finding.

### Complete Foundation runtime lifecycles and safe publication

- **Architecture and inspected risk surfaces:** Foundation owns application/bootstrap state, exception handling, CLI orchestration, maintenance mode, generated artifacts, HTTP test lifecycles, and most Laravel-facing application configuration. The audit covered every Foundation source and test file; its Auth, Console, Contracts, Filesystem, HTTP, Queue, Support, Testing, and Testbench boundaries; current Laravel source, tests, originating pull requests, and documentation; Orchestra Testbench lifecycle differences; Symfony Process and Console behavior; PHPUnit 13 teardown control flow; and the Swoole worker/coroutine ownership of every mutable state surface.

| ID | Category | Severity | Confidence | Failure and owning boundary | Final decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `foundation-05` | Defect | Minor | High | Deprecation bootstrap paths dereference an absent application/config repository, while a reporter failure can obscure the original process-level exception | Guard null/unbound bootstrap state and preserve the original exception through the existing report-only Swoole backstop |
| `foundation-06` | Defect | Major | High | One Foundation or Testbench setup/teardown callback failure skips independent cleanup and can strand framework statics or resources | Make every owned phase exhaustive and exactly-once, preserve the chronologically earliest throwable, and clear terminal state unconditionally |
| `foundation-07` | Defect | Major | High | External-service setup, Carbon clocks, dumper recursion guards, and `COLUMNS` restoration have duplicate or exception-unsafe process/coroutine ownership | Establish one test owner, restore exact prior process state in `finally`, and move dumper recursion state to class-specific coroutine context |
| `foundation-08` | Defect | Major | High | One Console termination phase failure skips application callbacks, duration handlers, or timestamp cleanup | Attempt every independent phase, retain the start timestamp through all handlers, clear it terminally, and rethrow the earliest failure |
| `foundation-09` | Defect and improvement | Major | High | Maintenance publication, decoding, deletion, cache writes, JSON negotiation, and post-commit side effects can expose corrupt state or false success; no honest in-process driver exists | Check every driver boundary, publish file state atomically, keep JSON requests out of stored HTML redirects/templates, separate committed state from later failures, and add the process-local array driver |
| `foundation-10` | Defect and userland footgun | Major | High | Namespace/Vite/native reads can violate declared contracts and worker-global configuration mutators lack lifecycle guidance | Validate native and JSON boundaries, cache only valid manifests, use merged typed configuration, and document boot-only shared mutators |
| `foundation-11` | Defect | Major | High | Exception renderer query retention is unbounded and disappearing Blade sources can replace the original exception | Bound count, SQL bytes, and bindings in existing coroutine context; treat mapping/read disappearance as a fallback condition |
| `foundation-12` | Defect | Major | High | Clear, view, storage-link, Composer, and small native commands report success without proving filesystem postconditions | Check delete/link/glob/read outcomes at each owner, sweep independent entries, and support broken symlinks without adding a generic deletion layer |
| `foundation-13` | Defect | Major | High | Config, route, event, and environment generation can destroy a prior valid artifact before the replacement validates | Build from source into isolated artifacts, validate fully, preserve existing modes, and atomically replace only at commit; new plaintext environment files use `0600` |
| `console-02` | Defect | Major | High | `GeneratorCommand` and Foundation generators ignore generated-file write failure and can emit success or create tests after an incomplete write | Add one protected mode-preserving atomic writer at the Console owner and route every base/view generator through it |
| `foundation-14` | Defect | Major | High | Publishers and installers ignore native copy/read/process/config failures or require an unusable TTY and continue as if installation completed | Stop at checked owner boundaries, use atomic small-file writes, require successful subprocesses, gate TTY by capability, and retain only the documented node-recovery warning path |
| `foundation-15` | Parity defect | Minor | High | `Dispatchable` bypasses Laravel's protected pending-dispatch construction seam | Port `newPendingDispatch()` and route every successful dispatch branch through late static binding |
| `queue-14` | Parity and upstream defect | Major | High | Applications cannot mark exceptions terminal through current Laravel's API, and upstream invokes narrow-typed callbacks for unrelated exceptions, causing `TypeError` | Port retry-stopping configuration end to end and invoke each callback only when the thrown exception matches its declared parameter type |
| `http-03` | Parity and correctness defect | Major | High | Preferred JSON, JSON health, `QUERY` helpers, public middleware bypass, and route-list paths are absent; empty diagnostic messages and native reflection `false` values break status/path truthfulness | Port the complete current public surfaces, retain explicit health state, and guard native filename boundaries |
| `auth-02` | Parity defect | Major | High | Explicit nullable guest redirects cannot be represented and a null target reaches redirect construction | Make guest redirects genuinely nullable through Auth/Foundation/facade surfaces and return a no-content 401 when no redirect resolves |
| `database-03` | Defect and parity defect | Major | High | Lazy refresh hooks run in the wrong coroutine/lifetime, runtime opt-out is ignored, and current assertion shapes are missing | Use the actual runtime coroutine flag, install hooks on every owned connection, make refresh state exception-safe, and port current multiple-row, iterable, and connection-aware assertions |
| `foundation-16` | Package metadata and documentation defect | Major | High | Foundation's split package omits direct dependencies and provenance while several new public APIs and runtime limitations are undiscoverable | Declare only direct split dependencies, add focused metadata coverage and provenance, and update seven task-first user guides plus the Kernel contract note |

- **Approved owner gates:** The owner approved the process-local/testing-only array maintenance driver, nullable redirect and current Laravel API additions, the truthful `Http\Kernel` contract expansion required by middleware configuration, the `0600` default for newly decrypted plaintext, and correcting verified upstream defects rather than preserving parity bugs. The Kernel contract change is documented for custom implementations. The existing worker maintenance wrapper remains a periodic same-process snapshot and does not make the array driver cross-process.
- **Important rejected concerns:** Do not add shutdown-callback registries, PHPUnit meta-fixtures, a generic finalizer, request-scoped Application/Vite clones, Carbon managers, locks, watchers, retry loops, PID incarnation tracking, publication/delete transaction services, arbitrary-stream copy transactions, SQL parsers, TTY emulation, or broad PHPStan impurity annotations. The supported paths are covered by existing lifecycle, coroutine-context, Filesystem replacement, Process, and typed-reflection primitives. Retain `Filesystem::delete()` cache clearing because the full caller set and PHP stat cache require it; use targeted cache invalidation only where raw command/native postconditions demand it.
- **Cross-package implications and revalidation:** Foundation revalidated all carried `view-01`, `testbench-01`, `reflection-01`, `reflection-02`, `config-01`, `config-02`, `context-01`, `context-04`, `coroutine-06`, `foundation-02`, `concurrency-01`, `concurrency-03`, `di-02`, `http-02`, `filesystem-07`, `foundation-04`, `events-01`, `events-04`, `events-06`, `foundation-01`, `support-02`, `encryption-03`, `server-process-10`, `bus-03`, `bus-17`, `bus-18`, `core-01`, and `core-05` assumptions. The later Queue work completed `queue-14` and revalidated Foundation's canonical base queue configuration under `queue-29`. `foundation-06` requires later full Testbench revalidation; `http-03` requires the HTTP audit; `auth-02` requires the Auth audit; and `database-03` requires the Database and Testbench audits.
- **Upstream and documentation:** Originating Laravel implementation and documentation pull requests supplied discovery history; current local Laravel default-branch source, tests, metadata, and docs supplied the porting reference. Hypervel deliberately retains its Swoole exception renderer, coroutine-aware test lifecycle, worker-cached maintenance model, and checked TTY behavior. Authentication, configuration, database testing, deployment, HTTP testing, middleware, and queue documentation now covers every new public call shape and important runtime limitation without exposing internal choreography.
- **Implementation:** Foundation/Testbench teardown now exhausts every independent owner and clears state exactly once. External-service clients, global clocks/environment, and dump recursion are restored at their actual lifetimes. Maintenance drivers, middleware, commands, Vite, renderers, clear/link/cache/environment commands, generators, publishers, and installers check native failures and publish only validated state. Current Laravel dispatch, retry-stopping, preferred-JSON, health, HTTP testing, route-list, nullable redirect, and database-testing APIs are complete; the narrow retry callback upstream defect is corrected. Dead duplicate Scout setup, stale helpers/properties/imports, unsafe raw boundaries, false success paths, and obsolete comments are removed.
- **Regression tests:** Deterministic coverage injects failure into every teardown/termination phase, setup wrapper shape, SDK/clock/dumper/environment restoration, maintenance/cache/file publication, generator/installer/publisher operation, reflection/Blade/native read, health state, retry callback typing, and lazy database refresh boundary. It also covers coroutine isolation, process-local array semantics, current Laravel public API call shapes, custom dispatch construction, Kernel contract consumers, mode preservation, old-artifact survival, and exact throwable precedence.
- **Performance and complexity:** Ordinary successful HTTP paths gain no lock, retry, registry, process lookup, or retained request state. Explicit JSON preference adds middleware only when enabled; maintenance adds narrow checks around calls already made; Vite validates cache misses; renderer bounds reduce memory; retry reflection runs only after a failed job; dispatch adds one protected static call; all publication and exhaustive lifecycle work is cold CLI, exception, termination, or test code. The rejected broad impurity sweep was removed, and the final design uses no workaround or speculative abstraction.
- **Laravel-facing result:** Current supported Laravel APIs and docs are restored while Hypervel's Swoole/coroutine ownership remains explicit. Intentional differences are limited to runtime-required behavior: process-local array maintenance guidance, capability-gated TTY, explicit health failure state, coroutine-aware testing, and checked native boundaries. Split-package metadata and custom Kernel requirements are truthful.
- **Validation and review:** Every changed test owner and affected group passed during implementation. The final `composer fix` gate changed no formatting, both PHPStan configurations passed, the complete components suite passed with 24,177 tests and 69,700 assertions, Testbench package mode passed with 365 tests and 1,087 assertions, and dogfood passed. `git diff --check`, broad stale-reference/native-boundary scans, a fresh full-diff caller/callee, lifecycle, API, documentation, performance, and overengineering self-review, and independent code review are complete with no remaining finding. The work is ready for owner pre-commit review.
- **Assessment:** Every verified Foundation lifecycle, publication, parity, metadata, documentation, and native-boundary defect is fixed at its lowest owner. Shared state is either boot-only, coroutine-local, process-local by explicit contract, or restored exactly. Prior valid artifacts survive failed generation, success is never emitted before its postcondition, and hot paths remain free of new synchronization or speculative machinery.

### Complete Console command, scheduling, and generator lifecycles

- **Architecture and inspected risk surfaces:** Console owns long-lived command prototypes, the worker-singleton Schedule and its mutable Event objects, nested command prompt configuration, coroutine-backed command execution, scheduled processes and output, signal cleanup, unique scheduled jobs, generator publication, and the Laravel-facing command and scheduling APIs. The audit covered every Console source and test file; Foundation's Schedule binding and generator consumers; Bus/Queue unique-job payload ownership; Database migration creation; Filesystem native boundaries; current Symfony Console behavior; current Laravel source, tests, documentation, and originating pull requests; and all carried `console-01`, `reflection-02`, `support-02`, and `console-02` assumptions.

| ID | Category | Severity | Confidence | Failure and owning boundary | Final decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `console-03` | Defect | Major | High | Schedule interval filters and `lastDayOfMonth()` freeze construction-time dates on the worker singleton | Evaluate intervals at use time and use cron's dynamic `L` day |
| `console-04` | Defect | Minor | High | `repeatEvery(0)` divides by zero and negative divisors can publish invalid repeat state | Require a positive sub-minute interval and document the truthful integer range |
| `console-05` | Defect | Major | High | CallbackEvent retains stale result/exception state and shares it across concurrent invocations | Store one initialized result/exception pair in coroutine context for the exact event run and forget it terminally |
| `console-06` | Defect | Major | High | Scheduled Event failures can skip output, callback, or mutex cleanup and later cleanup can mask the earliest throwable | Attempt every independent terminal phase, preserve the earliest throwable, and clear local mutex ownership in `finally` |
| `console-07` | Defect | Major | High | Completed scheduled system processes remain retained for the scheduler coroutine lifetime | Retain the exact process only through output/after callbacks and forget its object-keyed context slot on every terminal path |
| `console-08` | Defect | Major | High | Scheduled output ignores failed/partial writes and uses TOCTOU native reads that can violate string contracts | Require exact write lengths and centralize checked reads through Filesystem |
| `console-09` | Parity defect | Minor | High | Current Laravel scheduled callback dependency injection is absent and invokable filter callbacks are rejected | Port current Event callback parameter routing and widen filter callbacks to `bool\|callable` |
| `console-10` | Defect | Major | High | Isolated commands re-resolve their mutex for release, one terminal event failure skips cleanup, and setup-hook failure can retain signal handlers | Retain the exact mutex, make terminal phases exhaustive, and include supported trait setup in the command's signal-cleanup boundary |
| `console-11` | Defect | Minor | High | `CacheCommandMutex::exists()` reverses the answer while acquiring or releasing locks it does not own | Match CacheEventMutex's non-destructive closure-based probe |
| `console-12` | Defect | Major | High | Nested commands overwrite the parent command's process-global prompt configuration and restore only output on success | Reconfigure every parent prompt setting in `finally` and delete the partial restore helper |
| `console-13` | Defect and performance improvement | Major | High | OutputStyle eagerly exhausts iterables, duplicates memory, fabricates newlines, and tracks suppressed output incorrectly | Stream each item once, update state only after successful visible writes, and interpret Symfony output bitmasks exactly |
| `console-14` | Parity and type defect | Minor | High | Progress helpers call `count()` on generators | Infer totals only for countable iterables and preserve indeterminate progress otherwise |
| `console-15` | Parity and type defect | Minor | High | Console input getters, integer argument lookup, and completion metadata do not describe supported Symfony values | Restore mixed/array getter contracts, normalize the string lookup boundary, and accept iterable completion values |
| `console-16` | Parity addition | Minor | High | Current Laravel's CommandInput API and documentation are absent | Port the current source, tests, and task-first documentation while documenting the real argument-over-option collision behavior |
| `console-17` | Parity addition | Minor | High | Schedule environment filtering is absent | Port the current source, tests, and documentation without obsolete PHP polyfills |
| `console-18` | Intentional difference | Minor | High | Laravel's display-only cron timezone converter silently leaves common syntax unshifted, mishandles month boundaries, and freezes a DST offset | Resolve one expression timezone for due checks and display, retain the truthful raw expression and timezone, and do not port the unsound converter |
| `console-19` | Defect | Major | High | `schedule:test` permanently disables background execution on a worker-singleton Event | Delete the stale flag mutation; Hypervel owns background submission in ScheduleRunCommand, so direct Event runs are already synchronous and retain their original callback bindings |
| `console-20` | Improvement | Improvement | High | Optional command and scheduling lifecycle events are constructed and dispatched when no listener exists | Guard only the hot observational events with `hasListeners()` |
| `console-21` | Defect | Major | High | Object-ID registration loses a still-running same-Event owner when overlapping background invocations finish out of order | Reference-count the existing object-ID map without adding synchronization or another registry |
| `console-22` | Defect | Major | High | Scheduled unique jobs acquire locks without registering the exact payload metadata required for missing-model release | Register the transient dispatched job in the existing Bus sidecar and release only when catch-time consumption proves payload creation never began |
| `database-04` | Defect and parity defect | Major | High | Package migration generators publish a generic file before hooks, overwrite it unchecked, and lack current collision-free prefixes | Let MigrationCreator read the selected stub, publish the final migration atomically once, fire hooks afterward, and use the framework clock with transient path selection coroutine-local on its singleton |
| `console-23` | Test lifecycle defect | Minor | High | ScheduleListCommandTest clears an environment variable it does not own | Delete the stale teardown mutation |
| `console-24` | Metadata and documentation defect | Major | High | Console omits direct split dependencies, provenance, intentional differences, and guidance for accepted public APIs | Correct manifests and add concise task-first Command, scheduling, timezone, event-injection, and setup-trait guidance |
| `console-25` | Defect | Major | High | ScheduleRunCommand renders thrown runs from stale exit state, ignores observed non-zero background exits, and can mislabel overlap skips | Track failure and overlap state per run, report every observed non-zero exit, and render Failed, Skipped, or Finished from that run's real outcome |
| `console-26` | Test lifecycle defect | Minor | High | Scheduler and migration concurrency tests can wait forever when an owned coroutine fails before signaling | Bound every external-progress wait with a diagnostic failure while retaining deterministic interleavings |
| `reverb-04` | Test lifecycle defect | Minor | High | Deferred-webhook cancellation tests race live timers and sleeps instead of proving timer ownership | Inject the existing timer boundary and assert the exact timer IDs are cleared once |

- **Approved owner gates:** The owner approved the truthful raw-expression timezone display and additive `expression_timezone` JSON field instead of Laravel's incorrect converter, listener guards for optional observational events, and the additive optional MigrationCreator stub-path argument. These changes remove no Laravel API. The migration argument is backward-compatible, listener behavior remains visible to registered listeners and event fakes, and the timezone difference is limited to more accurate `schedule:list` presentation.
- **Unique-job ownership:** Connection and queue mutation occurs before lock acquisition. Console retains the exact lock and registers the exact transient cloned job in Bus's existing weak sidecar. If dispatch throws before payload creation consumes the entry, catch-time consumption both cleans the sidecar and proves lock release is safe. Once payload creation consumes the entry, Console conservatively leaves the lock owned by the queued or transport-ambiguous job; no per-driver commit protocol is added.
- **Migration publication:** Database MigrationCreator remains the sole existence-check, stub-read, populate, publish, and hook owner. Its optional path parameter avoids mutable singleton configuration and a duplicate public transaction. Current Laravel collision-free numbering is retained in coroutine context rather than a singleton property, and touched glob boundaries are checked through one shared helper used by the affected generator siblings.
- **Important rejected concerns:** Do not add a per-day schedule cache, cron compiler, universal timezone converter, prompt snapshot registry, output registry, generic finalizer, callback timeout, Termwind lock, group exception manager, per-event mutex-owner object, Queue transport commit protocol, mutable MigrationCreator configuration, generic native-boundary wrapper, or isolation for the single-writer `lastChecked` state and public compatibility snapshots. Do not add an upper repeat interval guard without a supported caller or fabricate native coroutine-run false handling. Retain Laravel's `ScheduledTaskFinished` then `ScheduledTaskFailed` ordering and dispatch-before-report sequence: reordering or shielding one throwable merely trades one reporting hole for another and changes established Sentry and Telescope sequencing.
- **Cross-package implications and revalidation:** Console revalidated the carried `console-01`, `reflection-02`, `support-02`, and `console-02` assumptions. The migration work is owned by `database-04` and requires the later full Database audit; deterministic deferred-webhook timer ownership is recorded as `reverb-04` for the later full Reverb audit.
- **Upstream and documentation:** Originating Laravel implementation and documentation pull requests supplied feature discovery, while current local Laravel source, tests, and docs supplied the porting reference. Hypervel retains its Swoole coroutine execution and background-scheduling ownership. Artisan and scheduling guides now document CommandInput, environment filters, expression timezones, event callback injection, and command setup traits without exposing internal lifecycle choreography.
- **Implementation:** Schedule and CallbackEvent state is isolated per run; Event and Command cleanup is exhaustive and owns exact mutexes and processes; scheduler status, same-Event registration, unique-job locks, output, and prompt restoration reflect the real invocation. Current CommandInput, environment filtering, callback injection, iterable progress, IO, and completion surfaces are complete. Generators publish atomically, MigrationCreator owns final collision-free publication through the framework clock, Reverb timer tests use exact injected ownership, and Console's split manifest and provenance are complete. Dead prompt restoration, stale schedule-test mutation, shallow-clone logic, eager output buffering, and obsolete teardown code are removed.
- **Performance and complexity:** No HTTP or server hot path changes. Correct interval filters perform three Carbon operations only when evaluated; callback/process runs use a small exact-object coroutine slot; running-event cleanup adds one integer count; optional-event guards normally remove allocations and dispatch; and exact output checks occur beside existing I/O. OutputStyle removes eager iterable buffering. There is no new lock, retry, polling, sleep, network call, unbounded retention, compatibility shim, or speculative abstraction.
- **Laravel-facing result:** Current CommandInput, Event callback injection, environment filtering, IO/completion types, progress handling, and migration collision behavior are restored. Existing Laravel call shapes remain compatible. The only deliberate presentation difference is rejecting Laravel's unsound display-only cron timezone conversion while retaining its correct timezone-aware due checks.
- **Regression strategy:** Cover dynamic interval dates and timezones, nullable schedule-timezone fallback, positive repeat validation, sequential and concurrent callback state, every Event and Command failure/cleanup ordering, exact mutex identity, retained process lifetime, checked output boundaries, callback injection, nested prompt restoration, iterable output/verbosity and progress generators, current parity APIs, mutation-free schedule testing with intact framework callback bindings, optional events with EventFake, run-local failure and overlap status, refcounted same-Event overlap, every unique-job pre/post-payload failure boundary, atomic final migration publication, framework-clock prefixes, collision prefixes, bounded concurrent-test progress, deterministic Reverb timer cancellation, complete split metadata, and stale environment ownership. Run every changed test file immediately, focused cross-package suites, then `composer fix`.
- **Validation and review:** Every changed test file and affected cross-package group passed during implementation. The final `composer fix` gate changed no formatting, both PHPStan configurations passed, and the complete parallel components, Testbench package, and dogfood suites passed. `git diff --check`, full dependency and stale-reference scans, fresh caller/callee, concurrency, resource, API, documentation, performance, and overengineering self-review, and independent code review are complete with no remaining finding. The work is ready for owner pre-commit review.
- **Assessment:** The approved design fixes verified lifecycle, parity, publication, and native-boundary defects at their existing owners. It uses current framework primitives, removes superseded helpers and unsafe state, and deliberately rejects broad machinery whose cost is not justified by a supported failure.

### Harden Server startup, reload, and process lifecycles

- **Architecture and inspected risk surfaces:** Server owns boot-time Swoole construction, listener registration, native startup, worker reload signaling, process titles, server configuration objects, and the split-package boundary used by Foundation, HTTP Server, Server Process, gRPC, Reverb, and Testbench. The audit covered every Server source and test file; all repository consumers and configuration writers; Foundation's stable Config reload and aggregate reload paths; Testbench's serve-command environment lifecycle; the carried `core-06` callback-signature constraint; current Hyperf historical source; and Swoole 6.2.2 construction, callback, manager-fork, PID-file, and signal behavior.

| ID | Category | Severity | Confidence | Failure and owning boundary | Final decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `server-01` | Defect | Major | High | Server ignores false from native settings, callback registration, listener creation, and startup, allowing configuration failure or a failed start to continue silently | Check each boolean/union native boundary and throw package `ServerException`; retain port settings' native void contract and classify unsupported Hypervel server types as package `InvalidArgumentException` |
| `server-02` | Defect | Critical | High | Reload casts invalid PID contents to zero or a negative value, which can signal a process group, then ignores the actual reload-signal result and reports success | Read once through Filesystem, validate one positive integer, check the actual required signals through a narrow protected boundary, and return failure for file/content/signal failures while missing configuration remains a typed fail-fast error |
| `server-03` | Defect | Major | High | The manager-cached process-title listener snapshots `app.name`, so replacement workers inherit a stale name after their stable Config repository is reloaded in place | Inject the Config contract and read the current application name at each cold process-lifecycle event |
| `server-04` | Defect | Major | High | Public ServerConfig and Port builders default to native mode and socket values Swoole rejects, while the advertised default HTTP type still triggers an undefined-key warning | Use `SWOOLE_PROCESS`, `SWOOLE_SOCK_TCP`, and the existing HTTP type as valid omitted-value defaults |
| `server-05` | Improvement | Improvement | High | Top-level `server.type` is an unconsumed Hyperf extension point while the real explicit custom-server seam requires the concrete Server class and an irrelevant constructor signature | Remove the dead config field and interface constructor requirement, and accept any `ServerInterface` in explicit factory injection |
| `server-06` | Package metadata defect | Major | High | Server imports undeclared Console, Filesystem, Foundation, Swoole, and POSIX surfaces while declaring an unused HTTP Server dependency that forms a split-package cycle | Depend on the Foundation application contract, declare only direct dependencies/extensions, remove HTTP Server, add metadata coverage, and record Hyperf provenance |
| `server-07` | Test lifecycle defect | Minor | High | Server and Testbench command tests delete environment values they do not own instead of restoring the exact process state they changed | Stop mutating APP_RUNNING_IN_CONSOLE environment state and restore every TESTBENCH_WORKING_PATH source exactly |
| `server-08` | Documentation defect | Minor | High | Deployment documents aggregate reload but the standalone server-only reload command and its truthful failure behavior are discoverable only from Reverb guidance | Add one concise `server:reload` paragraph to the existing deployment reload section |
| `server-09` | Defect | Minor | High | ServerConfig mixes global and package invalid-argument exceptions, so package-level catches miss some failures from the same configuration object | Standardize invalid configuration/property failures on the package exception |

- **Approved owner gates:** The owner approved removing the dead Hyperf-era top-level server-class configuration, deleting the interface constructor requirement, and widening explicit custom-server injection. Hyperf API parity is not required for this low-level package; no Laravel-facing API is affected.
- **Important rejected concerns:** Do not add a native-operation adapter, event whitelist, configure transaction, startup retry, PID registry or sidecar, process watcher, incarnation probe, lock, liveness poll, callback clone/factory, dual callback-signature adapter, pre-initialization guard, per-request state, or response-guard simplification. A crashed Swoole master can leave a stale PID whose later reuse cannot be excluded portably without disproportionate machinery. Foundation's best-effort reload helper already guards positive PIDs and intentionally has a different silent contract. The response fallback catch remains necessary because a user exception can throw from `__toString()`.
- **Implementation boundary:** Keep every correction on cold configure, startup, CLI reload, process lifecycle, package metadata, documentation, or test cleanup paths. Distinguish invalid configuration through package `InvalidArgumentException` from valid native operations that return failure through `ServerException`. Validate PID contents once, use the actual signal as the checked atomic boundary, and add only one protected signal wrapper for deterministic tests. Preserve the stable Config object so inherited replacement-worker listeners observe the refreshed application name.
- **Regression strategy:** Cover every false native startup boundary, valid and invalid builder defaults, omitted-type HTTP address overrides, package exception consistency, custom interface injection, missing configuration, every invalid PID shape, event/task signal success and failure, reload-aware process titles, exact environment restoration, direct split metadata, and the carried global/per-port `event_object` mutation paths. Run affected Server, Foundation, and Testbench files immediately, then the complete gate.
- **Performance and complexity:** No request or response hot path changes. Native checks occur beside existing boot calls; reload validation replaces unsafe probes; process titles add one typed Config lookup only when a process lifecycle event fires; and tests/metadata/docs have no runtime cost. The design adds no lock, retry, registry, polling, coroutine state, compatibility shim, or retained memory.
- **Laravel-facing result:** Server is Hyperf-derived Swoole infrastructure and has no Laravel API parity obligation. The approved API cleanup removes a nonfunctional Hyperf surface while making explicit custom implementation injection more flexible. Foundation's Laravel-shaped `serve` and aggregate `reload` commands retain their public call shapes.
- **Cross-package revalidation:** The carried `core-06` setting boundary remains complete: construction and every supported later global/per-port mutation reject truthy `event_object` before native publication. Foundation's stable Config identity, best-effort aggregate reload helper, gRPC listener insertion, Server Process boot listener, and Testbench serve wrapper remain compatible with the corrected Server contracts.
- **Implementation:** Server now fails immediately when native settings, callback registration, listener creation, or startup reports failure. Reload reads and validates one positive PID, checks the actual required signals, and reports success only after every configured worker group is signaled. Process titles read the stable Config repository at each lifecycle event; public builders have valid Swoole defaults; omitted HTTP types remain consistent through `serve` address overrides; and invalid configuration uses package exceptions. PHPStan discovers Swoole's authoritative Server signature, so the checked `Port|false` result narrows natively without the former imprecise `bool|Port` annotation. The dead top-level server-class field and interface constructor are removed while explicit custom implementations remain injectable. Split dependencies, provenance, deployment guidance, and exact test environment ownership are truthful.
- **Regression tests:** Deterministic native mocks cover every boolean/union failure boundary without adding production seams. Command tests cover missing configuration, unreadable and malformed PID files, positive validation, event/task signal success and failure, partial success, truthful output, and exit status. Additional coverage proves stable-repository title reloads, valid omitted defaults and address overrides, package exception consistency, custom interface construction, complete split metadata, environment restoration, and the carried global/per-port `event_object` guards.
- **Validation and review:** Every changed test file and the focused Server, Foundation config/reload, gRPC, Server Process, and Testbench group passed during implementation. The final `composer fix` gate changed no formatting, both PHPStan configurations passed, and the complete parallel components, Testbench package, and dogfood suites passed. Split Composer validation, `git diff --check`, broad stale-reference/dependency/config-classifier scans, a fresh caller/callee, API, lifecycle, performance, and overengineering self-review, and independent code review completed with no remaining finding.
- **Assessment:** All nine findings are resolved at their existing cold-path owners. No Laravel-facing call shape changed, the Hyperf-only API removal was explicitly approved, and the result adds no request state, synchronization, retry, registry, polling, compatibility shim, or hot-path cost. The residual stale-PID-reuse limitation is documented rather than hidden behind disproportionate process-incarnation machinery.

### Make WebSocket handshakes and connection callbacks lifecycle-safe

- **Architecture and inspected risk surfaces:** WebSocket Server is Hyperf-derived Swoole transport infrastructure whose worker-lifetime callback handler routes handshakes through Hypervel's HTTP stack, associates each verified Swoole session ID with one auto-singletoned handler class, stores per-connection values in a static session-keyed context, and sends locally or across BASE-mode workers through Swoole. The audit covered every package source and test file; Foundation's WebSocket kernel; Reverb's isolated router, handler, connection adapter, worker fan-out, and real BASE/PROCESS integration suites; HTTP Server's prepared response bridge; current Hyperf historical source; Swoole 6.2 custom-handshake, verified-session, callback-exception, connection-table, and pipe-message behavior; and the carried `websocket-server-01` and `http-server-06` boundaries.

| ID | Category | Severity | Confidence | Failure and owning boundary | Final decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `websocket-server-02` | Defect | Minor | High | Connected context deletion and filtered copying disagree with the class's dotted-key API, while null-valued keys are treated as absent and overwritten | Use dot-aware deletion, key-existence semantics, and null-preserving filtered copying while retaining the established missing-source no-op from `websocket-server-01` |
| `websocket-server-03` | Defect | Major | High | A handshake publishes its collector entry and deferred open callback before response emission succeeds, rejection can retain context, cancellation is rendered, and publication after a send-yield can race an immediate close | Keep state unpublished until successful prepared 101 emission, verify the session is still established, register the resolved handler's defer, publish without yielding, and clean every uncommitted exit through one commit-gated `finally` |
| `websocket-server-04` | Defect | Major | High | Message, open, and close resolution or event failures can escape native Swoole callbacks and fatally terminate a worker, while missing close metadata can skip context cleanup | Contain handler and optional-event failures independently through the application reporter with no-throw logging fallbacks, contain cancellation at unguarded Swoole callbacks while retaining guarded-handshake propagation, and release close-owned context deterministically even without a collector entry |
| `websocket-server-05` | Defect | Major | High | Sender initialization is unreachable, its public booleans report success without delivery or pipe acceptance, and native local failure incorrectly triggers cross-worker retries | Return verbatim local native results, distinguish absent local ownership with `?bool`, fan out only in BASE mode, aggregate actual pipe acceptance, read native worker state, and cache the server lazily |
| `websocket-server-06` | Defect | Minor | High | Handshake responses echo the client's complete offered subprotocol list even though RFC 6455 permits at most one explicitly selected protocol | Remove implicit selection and let application middleware set one supported response protocol |
| `websocket-server-07` | Defect | Minor | High | The base exception handler drops HTTP exception headers, exposes internal 500-class messages, misclassifies missing handlers as client errors, and uses a 400 last-resort fallback | Preserve `HttpExceptionInterface` status and headers, expose only sub-500 messages, classify missing handler classes as server failures, and use a generic 500 fallback |
| `websocket-server-08` | Package metadata and documentation defect | Minor | High | The split package omits direct Swoole and Symfony dependencies, provenance, metadata coverage, and user guidance for its public connection model | Correct the split manifest and metadata test, add provenance, and document task-first handler, context, sender, event, subprotocol, and Reverb usage |
| `websocket-server-09` | Improvement | Improvement | High | `FdCollector` allocates a wrapper object that duplicates its map key for every active connection | With owner approval, store the handler class string directly and delete the wrapper, removing roughly 9 MB of representative retained memory per 100,000 entries |
| `websocket-server-10` | Improvement | Improvement | High | Parent bootstrap ignores its router extension seam, forcing Reverb to duplicate the entire method while retaining an unread kernel property | With owner approval, bootstrap through `getRouter()`, keep the kernel local, and delete Reverb's duplicate override |
| `websocket-server-11` | Improvement | Improvement | High | Every successful local send formats and logs debug output, BASE fan-out logs per worker, and unrelated pipe messages resolve the package listener and Sender | With owner approval, remove send-path logging and guard message type before listener resolution |
| `websocket-server-12` | Improvement | Improvement | High | Two public Security constants preserve a misspelled identifier and noncanonical header spelling inherited from Hyperf | With owner approval, rename `PATTEN` to `PATTERN` without an alias and canonicalize the protocol header value |

- **Approved owner gates:** The owner approved deleting the per-connection wrapper, consolidating Reverb bootstrap through the existing router hook, removing send-path logging and unrelated pipe-listener resolution, and correcting both public constants without compatibility aliases. Hyperf API parity is not required; Laravel has no matching low-level package API.
- **Handshake ownership:** Resolve every potentially throwing prerequisite before emission, then emit through the prepared-request `ResponseBridge` boundary retained from `http-server-06`. A successful custom 101 response promotes the native connection to active state. The following verified `isEstablished()` session lookup closes the send-yield disconnect race; no yield occurs between that lookup, defer registration, collector publication, and the local commit flag. One authoritative `finally` releases collector/context state unless publication committed.
- **Sender semantics:** Swoole PROCESS mode has a shared verified connection table, so a missing direct lookup is authoritative and returns false. BASE mode keeps per-worker connection tables, so only that mode uses pipe fan-out; at least one recipient and acceptance from every `sendMessage()` call are required for true. A local native false remains false and is never retried elsewhere. Pipe acceptance is not delivery acknowledgment.
- **Important rejected concerns:** Do not add a connection generation, refcount, registry, state machine, acknowledgment protocol, retry, subprotocol registry, transient handler binding, duplicate real-server harness, remote-descriptor argument to `Context::forget()`, cosmetic context-key rename, generic callback guard, or compatibility alias. Retain consumer-less public primitives unless a concrete removal benefit exists.
- **Cross-package implications and revalidation:** Reverb keeps its isolated `getRouter()` override while deleting duplicated bootstrap logic. Its upstream-shaped void connection-send contract remains unchanged, and its separate channel-broadcast fan-out remains necessary for per-worker application state. Re-run Reverb's unit and real BASE/PROCESS multi-worker and cross-worker suites; no Reverb connection, protocol, or public API redesign is accepted.
- **Regression strategy:** Cover dotted deletion/copying and stored nulls; successful, failed, rejected, canceled, and disconnect-raced handshakes with prepared request emission; handler, event, resolution, application-reporter, logging-fallback, and contained message/close cancellation paths across open/message/close with deterministic cleanup; truthful local, dead, BASE fan-out, partial acceptance, frame, cache, and pipe-message sending; explicit and absent subprotocol selection; HTTP exception status/header/message behavior; split metadata; and Reverb router bootstrap plus real cross-worker delivery.
- **Performance and complexity:** The required `isEstablished()` call occurs once per successful handshake, and the server-mode property read occurs only after an absent direct Sender lookup. Normal sending removes container resolutions, formatting, and logging; context operations remain per explicit user call; collector retention falls materially. No request/message path gains a lock, registry, retry, acknowledgment, state machine, yield, poll, or unbounded state.
- **Laravel-facing result:** Laravel has no equivalent low-level WebSocket Server package, so its public API is unchanged. Hypervel's Reverb-facing contracts remain intact. The approved Hyperf-derived API cleanup removes only redundant or misspelled Hypervel-specific surfaces and preserves the useful handler, context, sender, event, and extension model.
- **Implementation:** WebSocket context now treats stored nulls and dotted keys consistently. Handshakes resolve their handler before emission, publish only after a successful prepared 101 response and native liveness check, and roll back every uncommitted exit. Unguarded message, close, and deferred-open callbacks contain cancellation and independent failures through the application exception handler with no-throw logging fallbacks, while the guarded handshake retains cancellation propagation. Sender reports native and pipe acceptance truthfully, fans out only in BASE mode, caches the native server lazily, and no longer performs send-path logging or unreachable worker-ID initialization. Collector entries are direct class strings; implicit subprotocol echo, the redundant per-connection wrapper, duplicated Reverb bootstrap, and obsolete listener are removed. HTTP exception fidelity, canonical security identifiers, split dependencies, provenance, and task-first public documentation are complete.
- **Regression tests:** Focused coverage proves dotted deletion and copying, stored nulls, transactional handshake success and every uncommitted exit, disconnect-race handling, callback/event/resolution/reporting failure containment, application reporting and logger fallback, message/close cancellation containment with close cleanup, truthful local and BASE/PROCESS sending, partial pipe acceptance, server caching, listener filtering, explicit subprotocol selection, HTTP exception fidelity, router-hook bootstrap, canonical security constants, and split metadata. Deferred-open tests retain exact child coroutine IDs and use one bounded monotonic completion wait that includes native defers, so child failure produces a descriptive test failure instead of stranding ParaTest. Reverb's focused and real BASE/PROCESS multi-worker and cross-worker coverage remains green.
- **Validation and review:** Every changed test file and the complete WebSocket Server group pass. Reverb's focused and real integration matrix was revalidated. The final `composer fix` gate changed no formatting, both PHPStan configurations passed, and the complete parallel components, Testbench package, and dogfood suites passed. Split Composer validation, `git diff --check`, stale-reference scans, package-checklist parity, and a fresh caller/callee, callback, resource, API, documentation, hot-path, retained-memory, and overengineering review are complete. Independent code review identified and then verified the Swoole 6.2 cancellation boundary and restored application-reporter layer before signing off with no remaining finding.
- **Assessment:** All accepted findings are resolved at their lowest owners. Successful handshakes add one necessary native liveness lookup, while normal sends remove container resolutions, formatting, logging, and per-connection wrapper allocation. Failure reporting and cancellation checks run only on exceptional paths. The result adds no lock, registry, retry, acknowledgment, state machine, timeout policy, compatibility shim, production poll, or unbounded worker state, and leaves no stale transport or bootstrap path.

### Make Watcher drivers and managed processes lifecycle-safe

- **Architecture and inspected risk surfaces:** Watcher is Hyperf-derived development infrastructure. One driver coroutine reports changed paths through a bounded channel while `Watcher` debounces restarts. Find, Find Newer, and Scan File poll; Fswatch owns a long-lived subprocess. `ServerRestartStrategy` owns an array-form `proc_open` child, while Horizon supplies a sibling restart strategy. The audit covered every Watcher source and test file; all repository callers, listeners, configuration, package metadata, and documentation; Support's dotenv manager; Foundation's dotenv reload listener and server configuration; Horizon's process strategy and manifest; current Hyperf historical source; GNU, BusyBox, and macOS find/fswatch behavior; and Swoole channel and coroutine lifecycle semantics.

| ID | Category | Severity | Confidence | Failure and owning boundary | Final decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `watcher-01` | Defect | Minor | High | The command boundary advertises an array while Swoole can return false, and retains dead non-Swoole fallback code in a Swoole-only framework | Require Swoole explicitly, remove the fallback, and convert native false into a named runtime failure |
| `watcher-02` | Defect | Major | High | BusyBox detection reads the wrong stream, so unsupported fractional `find -mmin` values can make every scan fail silently | Detect GNU find positively through `find --version`, fall back to integer minutes, inject the established stdout logger into both find drivers, and warn on failed scan ticks while still processing valid stdout |
| `watcher-03` | Defect | Major | High | One missing configured root invalidates a combined scan, a file changed in the starting second is permanently skipped, and `filemtime()` false is not handled | Normalize targets centrally, filter absent polling targets on every tick, skip empty scans, use the correct strict time boundary, and ignore files whose metadata disappeared during inspection |
| `watcher-04` | Defect | Major | High | Find Newer launches background shell commands whose output interleaves and whose exit statuses are not authoritative, then advances its cutoff after an incomplete scan | Execute one escaped multi-target find per group, retain valid output from failed commands, and promote the prepared reference cutoff only after every group succeeds |
| `watcher-05` | Defect | Major | High | Scan File suppresses simultaneous modifications when any deletion occurs, never emits deletions, and aborts later roots when one configured directory disappears | Emit additions, modifications, and deletions independently; tolerate only per-root directory disappearance; and remove the false manual-restart warning |
| `watcher-06` | Rejected concern | — | High | The macOS fswatch command appears non-recursive because Hypervel does not pass `-r` | Make no change: the default FSEvents monitor is recursive and `-r` is documented as a no-op there |
| `watcher-07` | Defect | Minor | High | Trailing-slash directory entries and root-level glob entries derive inconsistent targets and prefixes | Use one shared target normalizer across all drivers and normalize the directory prefix in `WatchPath::matches()` without changing configured values |
| `watcher-08` | Improvement | Improvement | High | The idle coordinator wakes about 1,000 times per second and retains every filename during a continuous change burst although restart state is boolean | With owner approval, close the result channel when the driver terminates, block indefinitely while idle, retain the 1 ms debounce only after a change, and track one pending-restart boolean |
| `watcher-09` | Defect | Major | High | The restart strategy queues one coroutine per change behind a token and reconstructs ownership from a mutable PID file, allowing stale queued launches, redundant serial relaunches, and relaunch after final stop | Replace token serialization with one owner coroutine, retain the exact direct-child PID from `proc_get_status()`, coalesce restart demand in a boolean, and make final stop reject late restart demand while preserving deterministic reap |
| `watcher-10` | Defect | Major | High | Deleting `.env` leaves stale inherited values because reload callers skip absent files and the shared reload boundary cannot publish an authoritative empty environment; Horizon never reloads before respawn | Make `DotenvManager::reload()` remove prior keys, flush the repository, clear its cache, and `safeLoad()` unconditionally; update Foundation and Watcher callers; reload through the application immediately before each Horizon child spawn |
| `watcher-11` | Improvement | Improvement | High | A Hyperf-derived restart event and listener form an unused public observer layer for an invariant now owned directly by process creation | With owner approval, delete the event, listener, provider wiring, and obsolete tests instead of preserving an unconsumed compatibility surface |
| `watcher-12` | Package metadata, configuration, and documentation defect | Minor | High | Split manifests omit direct dependencies, daemon mode is only supplied through call-site defaults, provenance is absent, and driver deletion behavior is undocumented | Correct Watcher and touched Horizon manifests, declare non-daemon server mode in public base configuration, remove call-site defaults, add provenance, and document each driver's observable change types concisely |

- **Process ownership:** One owner coroutine performs `proc_open()`, records the exact direct-child PID before any wait, and retains it until `proc_close()` completes. Restart and stop read and signal that published PID without yielding after the read. A restart requested before PID publication is acted on immediately after publication; repeated requests coalesce; final stop clears pending restart and makes later restart calls no-ops until an explicit start. Process creation failure resets lifecycle ownership so a later change can retry. Environment reload occurs in the owner immediately before every child creation.
- **Driver boundaries:** Find-family drivers resolve root paths consistently and filter missing targets at each polling scan; Fswatch deliberately retains configured absent roots because a long-lived event monitor cannot adopt a target omitted at startup. Failed find ticks warn but do not discard valid output. Find Newer advances its two-file reference role only after the complete scan succeeds. Scan File catches only Symfony's directory-not-found exception at the individual directory boundary.
- **Shared dotenv boundary:** Reload treats an absent file as an authoritative empty environment while malformed files still throw. The reachable Watcher restart failure remains visible through the coroutine exception reporter; owner state is cleared, and the next file change retries. No special catch, retry loop, or error-swallowing path is added. Horizon reloads from the application's configured environment path and filename at the existing pre-spawn boundary.
- **Owner approvals:** The owner approved the blocking-channel/boolean-pending coordinator improvement; deletion of the unused restart event and listener surface; and explicit `server.settings.daemonize = false` base configuration. The first materially reduces idle CPU and retained burst memory. The second removes an unconsumed Hyperf-specific observer API without affecting the public restart strategy extension point. The third makes an existing process-ownership requirement truthful in configuration rather than hidden in call-site defaults.
- **Important rejected concerns:** Do not add deletion snapshots to find drivers, retries, adaptive timeouts, sentinels, configurable debounce, NUL-delimited output machinery, PID sidecars, incarnation registries, readiness handshakes, process abstractions, signal escalation, scan hashing optimizations, Hyperf AST reload, or a special malformed-dotenv catch. Do not filter absent roots from Fswatch. Retain the existing bounded WaitGroup cleanup join and outer channel close because they release real ownership.
- **Implemented changes:** Watcher now normalizes targets once, treats native execution failure truthfully, detects GNU find positively, reports failed scan exit codes without discarding stdout, filters missing polling targets, preserves Find Newer's cutoff after incomplete scans, reports every Scan File change type, and blocks while idle with boolean restart demand. One owner coroutine retains the exact watched child PID only until reap, coalesces restart demand, honors pre-publication restart/stop, rejects late restart calls after final stop, and reloads the environment before each spawn. Support publishes absent environment files as empty state, Foundation uses that shared boundary unconditionally, and Horizon reloads through the configured application environment before each child creation. Obsolete event/listener/coordinator code is removed; configuration, split manifests, provenance, and driver documentation are current.
- **Cross-package implications and revalidation:** `watcher-10` is owned by Support's dotenv boundary and affects Watcher, Foundation, and Horizon. Both existing reload callers and Horizon's start/restart lifecycle were revalidated. Watcher metadata adds its direct runtime dependencies; Horizon metadata adds only dependencies proven by current direct imports.
- **Regression tests:** Focused coverage proves GNU and non-GNU capability detection, diagnostic non-zero exits with valid stdout, missing and late-created roots, exact-start-second changes, metadata disappearance, incomplete scans without cutoff promotion, simultaneous add/modify/delete changes, per-root directory disappearance, normalized root and trailing-slash patterns, blocking idle/debounce/terminal channel behavior, restart coalescing, pre-publication restart/stop, final stop and late restart rejection, creation and cleanup failure recovery, exact-PID signaling and unpublication immediately after reap, absent and malformed dotenv reloads, Horizon pre-spawn reload, configuration, split metadata, and documented driver behavior.
- **Performance and complexity:** No request or application hot path changes. Per-poll target existence checks are linear in configured roots and negligible beside filesystem scans. Find Newer launches fewer subprocesses. The approved coordinator improvement removes idle polling and unbounded filename retention. Process and environment work remains development-only and cold. No lock, registry, retry loop, configurable policy, or general process state machine is introduced.
- **Validation and review:** Every changed and new focused Watcher, Support, Foundation, and Horizon test passed. The final `composer fix` gate changed no formatting, both PHPStan configurations passed, and the complete parallel components, Testbench package, and dogfood suites passed. Both changed split manifests validate, `git diff --check` is clean, package-checklist parity holds, and broad stale-reference scans are clean. Fresh self-review covered driver output and filtering, channel termination, child ownership and every state transition, dotenv callers, configuration, documentation, retained state, hot-path cost, and overengineering. Independent code review corrected post-reap PID unpublication and scan-warning diagnostics, then signed off with no remaining finding.
- **Assessment:** All accepted defects and improvements are resolved at their lowest owners. The result deletes more machinery than it adds, improves idle CPU and burst memory use, launches fewer subprocesses, and adds only negligible target-existence checks beside polling scans. It introduces no request hot-path work, lock, retry loop, registry, timeout policy, compatibility shim, speculative extension point, or retained filename/process history.
- **Laravel-facing result:** Watcher has no Laravel counterpart. Foundation's Laravel-facing APIs remain unchanged; the configuration addition makes the existing Swoole-only non-daemon requirement explicit. Horizon's public process behavior remains intact. The owner approved removing the unused Hyperf-derived event layer while preserving useful Watcher and Horizon extension surfaces.

### Complete Database persistence lifecycles and current Laravel parity

- **Architecture and inspected risk surfaces:** Database is a Laravel-derived query, schema, migration, Eloquent, pooled-connection, testing, and transaction package adapted to long-lived Swoole workers. The work also covers the Core task-terminal and Server pre-fork boundaries, Redis's corresponding pooled ownership, Foundation/Testing/Testbench SQLite consumers, and Scout/NestedSet model-boot consumers. The audit covered every Database source and test file; all repository consumers of connection resolution, transactions, SQLite classification, model boot, and the accepted APIs; current Laravel 13.x source, tests, documentation, and originating pull requests; Hyperf's Redis deferred-release correction; native SQLite, PDO, phpredis, and Swoole behavior; split metadata; and every carried Database/Redis dependency recorded by the routing index. The detailed implementation design is recorded in [`2026-07-24-1013-database-persistence-lifecycles-and-current-laravel-parity.md`](2026-07-24-1013-database-persistence-lifecycles-and-current-laravel-parity.md).

| ID | Category | Severity | Confidence | Failure and owning boundary | Final decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `database-05` | Defect | Major | High | Default non-coroutine Swoole tasks retain exact pooled wrappers and their raw context, transaction, and session state across tasks | Publish `TaskTerminated`, retain exact wrappers in the pooled resolver, and release them only at that terminal task boundary |
| `database-06` | Defect | Major | High | Parent-created pools, PDOs, sockets, timers, channels, and fallback context can cross Swoole forks | Publish one final `BeforeServerFork` boundary and independently discard resolved exact leases and flush already-resolved Database and Redis pools before fork and at child start |
| `database-07` | Defect | Major | High | Multiple wrappers advertise independent ownership of one physical in-memory SQLite PDO | Normalize valid in-memory pool capacity to one and reuse the existing pool channel as the sole ownership primitive |
| `database-08` | Defect and parity defect | Major | High | Inconsistent literal checks miss valid SQLite URI-memory forms, reject valid file URIs, and can truncate a URI string instead of the attached file | Centralize native-faithful classification in `SQLiteDatabase`, route every live consumer through it, and refresh the canonical attached path |
| `database-09` | Defect, parity defect, and upstream defect | Major | High | Physical PDO state, logical levels, manager records, callbacks, events, retries, disconnects, and pooled reuse can disagree or replace the primary failure | Separate transaction phases, publish state only after physical success, detach records before callbacks, exhaust rollback cleanup, retry only after complete cleanup, and preserve the earliest operation failure |
| `database-10` | Defect, parity defect, and upstream defect | Major | High | Eloquent publishes `$booted` before boot finishes, so recursion observes incomplete state, exceptions poison publication, and siblings race | Track the exact boot owner and serialize only incomplete first publication with the existing model-class Mutex |
| `database-11` | Supported current Laravel parity | Major/Minor | High | Current supported Query, Eloquent, Schema, migration, connector, provider, exception, and metadata behavior is missing or stale | Port the complete supported current Laravel surface discovered through its originating source and documentation changes |
| `database-12` | Performance improvement | Improvement | High | Ordinary attribute reads merge every cached cast and raw SQL rendering repeatedly reindexes bindings | Port Laravel's per-key cast merge and indexed binding substitution, including truthful resource rendering |
| `database-13` | Package metadata and documentation defect | Major | High | Split dependencies, provenance, deliberate deprecated omissions, and accepted public APIs are incomplete or undiscoverable | Correct Database and Redis manifests, add metadata coverage and omission markers, and update concise task-first guides |
| `database-14` | Contract defect | Minor | High | The connection contract cannot express rollback to a transaction level even though the implementation supports it and Queue requires it | Add the implementation's optional rollback-level parameter to the contract |
| `redis-03` | Cross-package defect | Major | High | Non-coroutine same-connection commands register an invalid defer, while repeated callback operations retain one no-op defer per call despite immediate wrapper release | Register no outside-coroutine defer and at most one owner-ID terminal defer per pool/coroutine while preserving immediate callback-form release |
| `redis-04` | Cross-package defect | Major | High | Abandoned native MULTI or PIPELINE clients can be requeued and silently queue commands for a later borrower | Inspect local phpredis mode before reuse and discard terminally when queueing |
| `redis-05` | Cross-package defect | Major | High | Redis sockets, heartbeat timers, pools, and pinned fallback state share Database's fork-inheritance defect | Consume the shared process lifecycle through exact proxy discard plus already-resolved pool flush |
| `redis-06` | Cross-package defect | Major | High | A throwing command-event listener can skip ownership handoff or cleanup and leak the borrowed pool slot | Complete command, event, and cleanup phases independently while retaining established failure precedence |
| `redis-07` | Cross-package defect | Major | High | `WATCH` returns its client before `MULTI`/`EXEC`, and callback-form completion bypasses wrapper watch-state settlement | Pin WATCH to its exact wrapper, track only unobservable native watch state, settle successful terminal commands, and discard abandoned state |
| `redis-08` | Cross-package parity and type defect | Major | High | Laravel-facing `Redis::discard()` collides with Pool's lifecycle `discard(): void`, destroys the wrapper, and never invokes native DISCARD | Route only the Redis command through a distinct internal native-discard method while retaining Pool's lifecycle contract |

- **Task and process ownership:** `TaskCallback` now publishes a guarded terminal event after action/finish work and preserves the earliest failure. `Server::start()` publishes one final pre-fork event immediately before native start. Database's resolver and Redis's existing proxies own their exact borrowed identities; lifecycle listeners aggregate only already-resolved owners, detach context before I/O, continue independent cleanup after failure, and discard rather than release inherited resources. No generic lifecycle registry, priority mechanism, reflective context deletion, or reconstructed configured-name cleanup remains.
- **Redis ownership and native state:** Each pool/coroutine owns one terminal defer identified by the owning coroutine ID, so copied context cannot inherit false defer ownership and repeated callback-form operations cannot accumulate closures. Callback transactions still return newly pinned wrappers immediately, including in long-lived coroutines. The wrapper reads phpredis's local queue mode at release and tracks one boolean only for WATCH because phpredis exposes no corresponding getter. Native DISCARD has one explicit route around Pool's method-name collision. Ordinary Redis commands add no defer lookup or network command.
- **SQLite and Testbench ownership:** `SQLiteDatabase` is the sole lowercase URI and in-memory classifier across connectors, pools, schema state, RefreshDatabase, parallel testing, the shared integration harness, and Testbench. Automatic parallel database management and file create/drop reject non-memory URI names rather than treating them as PHP paths. File refresh uses the canonical attached database and preserves its inode. Mixed RefreshDatabase connection sets cache only physical memory PDOs, and unsplit base/read/write aliases share one canonical wrapper around one memory PDO. Tests retain assigned ParaTest worker identity and use bounded wait paths.
- **Transaction truthfulness:** Begin, callback/pre-commit, physical commit, logical publication, manager callbacks, events, rollback, disconnect, and retry are explicit fixed phases. Manager records detach before user rollback callbacks and rollback callbacks exhaust deepest-first; commit callbacks retain current Laravel's stop-on-first contract. Cleanup failure prevents retry and never replaces the primary callback, listener, commit, or synthesized nested-deadlock failure. A lost physical failure during disconnect means the session is already terminal, so manager cleanup and reconnection continue and any manager failure remains observable; non-lost physical failures retain their existing primacy and preserve truthful state. No state machine, transaction object, ambiguity model, callback registry, generic finalizer, or retry framework is introduced.
- **Eloquent publication and current parity:** First model boot remains lock-free after stable publication, permits same-owner construction only after `$booted` is true, clears failed pre-publication ownership for retry, and retains publication through post-boot hooks. Scout and NestedSet no longer instantiate an unpublished model inside boot. Current supported Laravel Query, Eloquent, Schema, migration, connector, provider, exception, facade, and PHPDoc surfaces are ported from current source after originating-PR discovery. SQL Server, dynamic/direct connection, directly deprecated compatibility, and other approved unsupported surfaces remain omitted and explicitly recorded where future syncs look.
- **Important rejected concerns:** Do not add a generic task cleanup registry, event priorities, transaction state machine, shared SQLite cache or second lock, URI rewriting for automatic parallel isolation, Redis queue-mode mirror, eager-release marker, per-pin defer, `WeakMap` defer registry, transaction abstraction, boot registry, compatibility shims, benchmarks for source-proven performance improvements, or documentation that exposes internal lifecycle choreography. Deliberate Laravel-style escape hatches remain escape hatches. Hyperf parity is not a requirement; only the bounded Redis lifecycle lesson from its fix is retained in the cleaner owning shape.
- **Performance and compatibility:** Normal Database queries gain no container lookup, context operation, lock, retry, logging, yield, or registry. Stable model construction adds one static owner-map `isset()`, and first boot alone reads the coroutine ID and may use the existing Mutex. Redis release adds one local extension-state read and one boolean; same-connection publication adds one owner-ID comparison only when publishing a pin. Per-key cast merging and indexed raw-SQL substitution remove work. SQLite normalization occurs at setup, lifecycle events are cold task/start boundaries, and exhaustive cleanup is exceptional or terminal. Public Laravel APIs remain intact; supported current APIs are restored, while internal Swoole adaptations remain at their lowest owners.
- **Implementation:** Added exact task and pre-fork lifecycle events and Database/Redis listeners; replaced reflective task cleanup with exact resolver/proxy ownership; deduplicated Redis terminal defers without delaying callback release; made Redis event cleanup, WATCH, native DISCARD, mode checks, purge, and fork cleanup truthful; centralized SQLite classification and serialized shared-memory ownership; corrected RefreshDatabase and parallel/Testbench consumers; repaired transaction publication, retry, rollback, callback, disconnect, and query-log state; made first Eloquent boot coroutine-safe; ported the complete accepted current Laravel Database surface and both approved performance corrections; made the connection contract expose the implementation's existing rollback-level parameter; completed split metadata, provenance, omission markers, facade metadata, and concise user documentation; and removed every superseded listener, literal classifier, dependency, comment, and test assumption.
- **Regression tests:** Deterministic coverage spans task and fork failure precedence; exact Database and Redis wrapper ownership; copied-context and callback-immediate Redis release; real MULTI/PIPELINE/WATCH/DISCARD state; full SQLite URI classification, canonical refresh, mixed RefreshDatabase ownership, and one-owner concurrency; transaction begin/commit/rollback/disconnect and manager-callback failures; Queue's typed connection consumer rolling back a failed-job transaction to level zero; retry suppression and primary-failure preservation; recursive, concurrent, failed, and post-publication model boot; all supported current Query, Eloquent, Schema, migration, connector, provider, exception, metadata, facade, documentation-facing call shapes, and external MySQL, MariaDB, PostgreSQL, SQLite, Redis, and Valkey behavior.
- **Cross-package revalidation:** The work closes carried `database-01` through `database-04`, `redis-01` and `redis-02`, `pool-04`, `pool-05`, `pool-08`, `context-04`, `support-02`, `foundation-06`, and the Database side of `database-03`. Core and Server own the new lifecycle producers; the full Redis audit revalidated `redis-03` through `redis-08`; Foundation, Testing, and Testbench consume the SQLite and transaction-test boundaries; Scout and NestedSet consume the corrected model-boot publication; Telescope's aggregate SQL expectation follows the corrected grammar. The later Queue audit added `database-14` and revalidated the rollback-level contract through its failed-job timeout cleanup. Focused consumer coverage and the complete gate remain green.
- **Validation and review:** Every changed test file and affected package group passed during implementation. The final `composer fix` gate changed no formatting, both PHPStan configurations passed, and the complete parallel components, Testbench package, and dogfood suites passed. Database and Redis split manifests validate, `git diff --check`, package-checklist parity, broad stale-reference/classifier/dependency scans, and a fresh full-diff caller/callee, lifecycle, transaction, API, documentation, hot-path, retained-state, and overengineering self-review are complete. Independent post-implementation code review is signed off with no remaining findings.
